class="cls-1" points="133.38 53.6 133.38 20.95 572.62 20.95 572.62 53.6"></polyline><polyline class="cls-1" points="572.62 333.2 572.62 365.85 133.38 365.85 133.38 333.2"></polyline></svg><h4 class="mt-2 font-itc-avant-garde-gothic-pro text-sm md:text-base font-light">Decrypt’s Art, Fashion, and Entertainment Hub.</h4><hr class="mt-2 bg-scene min-w-full border-0" style="height:1px"/><a class="mt-2 inline-flex items-center align-middle py-2 space-x-2 font-akzidenz-grotesk leading-none text-sm font-medium hover:opacity-50" href="/scene"><span>Discover SCENE</span><svg aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" data-prefix="fas" data-icon="long-arrow-right" class="svg-inline--fa fa-long-arrow-right fa-w-14 " role="img" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" viewBox="0 0 448 512"><path fill="currentColor" d="M254.544 154.427L320.118 220H24c-13.255 0-24 10.745-24 24v24c0 13.255 10.745 24 24 24h296.118l-65.574 65.573c-9.373 9.373-9.373 24.569 0 33.941l16.971 16.971c9.373 9.373 24.569 9.373 33.941 0L440.97 272.97c9.373-9.373 9.373-24.569 0-33.941L305.456 103.515c-9.373-9.373-24.569-9.373-33.941 0l-16.971 16.971c-9.373 9.372-9.373 24.568 0 33.941z"></path></svg></a></div></div><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Ransomware turns 30 this month. And the malicious software, invented by the well meaning but wacko evolutionary biologist Joseph L. Popp, is thriving. </span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Attacks spiked by </span><a href="https://www.mcafee.com/enterprise/en-us/assets/reports/rp-quarterly-threats-aug-2019.pdf" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">118%</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> during the first quarter of this year, with </span><a href="https://blog.barracuda.com/2019/08/28/threat-spotlight-government-ransomware-attacks" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">hackers singling out for punishment</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> state and local governments, while continuing to target </span><a href="https://www.cnet.com/news/wannacry-ransomware-real-victim-small-business-local-corner-store/" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">businesses</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">, </span><a href="https://www.cnet.com/news/us-charges-iranian-hackers-over-ransomware-attacks-on-major-cities/" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">universities</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">, and </span><a href="https://threatpost.com/alabama-hospitals-pay-up-ransomware-attack/148937/" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">hospitals</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">.</span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Ransomware’s robust health is due to three symbiotic factors: our increasing reliance on digitization; ever more sophisticated crooks delivering more powerful viral strains, and the prevalence of untraceable ransoms—now almost always paid in bitcoin or other cryptocurrencies. </span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Hackers’ demands are also increasing along with the chilling efficacy of their product. According to ransomware recovery specialists </span><a href="https://www.coveware.com/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow external noopener" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">Coveware</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">, the average ransom payment </span><a href="https://www.coveware.com/blog/2019/7/15/ransomware-amounts-rise-3x-in-q2-as-ryuk-amp-sodinokibi-spread?utm_source=emsisoft" target="_blank" rel="nofollow external noopener" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">increased by 184%</span></a> <span style="font-weight:400"> in the first half of 2019. Largely, that’s thanks to an increasing number of attacks with new ransomware strains such as RYUK on large enterprises. </span><a href="https://www.datto.com/uk/blog/a-look-at-ransomware-in-2019" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">The average ransom demanded</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">, internationally, is now $4,300. </span></p><div class="post-content-w-full my-8 flex xl:hidden items-center justify-center"><div class="relative flex-none flex" style="min-width:300px;min-height:250px"></div></div><div class="post-content-w-full my-10 hidden xl:flex items-center justify-center"><div class="relative flex-none flex" style="min-width:300px;min-height:250px"></div></div><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Desperate for a quick solution, most victims pay up, data recovery professionals told </span><i><span style="font-weight:400">Decrypt</span></i><span style="font-weight:400">. In fact, </span><a href="https://on.code42.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Code42-Data-Exposure-Report-2-v11.pdf?utm_source=emsisoft" target="_blank" rel="nofollow external noopener" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">according to one report</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">, many businesses have begun hoarding cryptocurrencies, in case of an attack. Is it any wonder then that </span><a href="https://blog.emsisoft.com/en/33977/is-ransomware-driving-up-the-price-of-bitcoin/" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">some analysts</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> believe major ransomware attacks could be affecting the price of cryptocurrency? </span></p><h2 class="sc-b2a202e4-2 bmropA gg-dark:text-white scene:font-itc-avant-garde-gothic-pro scene:font-light" style="margin-top:2em;text-align:left;padding-bottom:16px;margin-bottom:16px;border-bottom:1px solid #dfe2e4" color="#333"><span style="font-weight:400">Happy Birthday ransomware</span></h2><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Ransomware refers to the category of computer viruses that are designed to quickly across computer networks and encrypt the files on them; the idea is to hold sensitive documents hostage until the victim pays ransom to the hacker. </span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">The vulnerability of those targeted—</span><a href="https://decrypt.co/12481/bitcoin-ransomware-paralyzes-nursing-homes-across-the-us" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">nursing homes</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">, </span><a href="https://decrypt.co/10389/ransomware-attacks-targeting-local-infrastructure-new-research-shows" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">providers of local infrastructure</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">, and </span><a href="https://decrypt.co/7651/second-florida-city-gives-in-to-bitcoin-ransom-pays-out-500000" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">cities</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">—gives them little alternative. In May, an RYUK attack on the City of Riviera Beach, Florida, </span><a href="https://statescoop.com/florida-city-pays-hackers-600000-after-ransomware-attack/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow external noopener" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">forced the local government to cough up $600,000</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> to decrypt the frozen files. In October, </span><a href="https://thenextweb.com/hardfork/2019/10/25/hackers-give-johannesburg-government-3-days-to-pay-over-30k-in-bitcoin/" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">hackers hit </span></a><span style="font-weight:400">the administrative website of the City of Johannesburg, in South Africa, and threatened to publish the stolen data on the Internet—unless they received a $30,000 bitcoin ransom. The city </span><a href="https://threatpost.com/johannesburg-refuses-ransom-pay/149676/" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">refused</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> to pay.</span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">But as bad as the blight is, ransomware wasn’t born bad. </span></p><div class="post-content-w-full my-8 flex xl:hidden items-center justify-center"><div class="relative flex-none flex" style="min-width:300px;min-height:250px"></div></div><div class="post-content-w-full my-10 hidden xl:flex items-center justify-center"><div class="relative flex-none flex" style="min-width:300px;min-height:250px"></div></div><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Harvard-educated Popp, its inventor, was a polymath, and ransomware was born in 1989 out of his desire to combat AIDS, or </span><a href="https://www.theatlantic.com/technology/archive/2016/05/the-computer-virus-that-haunted-early-aids-researchers/481965/" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">so he claimed</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">. In his misguided determination to amass funds to thwart the disease, he mailed more than 20,000 infected floppy disks to the delegate list of a World Health Organisation forum. When the recipients ran the disks, their computers froze, and an onscreen message instructed them to send funds to access a second disk that would restore their files. </span></p><figure class="w-full max-w-full mt-4 overflow-hidden"><img alt="Joseph L. Popp" loading="lazy" width="240" height="328" decoding="async" data-nimg="1" class="object-contain object-center w-full" style="color:transparent" sizes="(min-width: 640px) 950px, 384px" srcSet="https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:16:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 16w, https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:32:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 32w, https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:48:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 48w, https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:64:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 64w, https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:96:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 96w, https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:128:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 128w, https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:256:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 256w, https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:384:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 384w, https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:640:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 640w, https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:750:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 750w, https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:828:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 828w, https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:1080:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 1080w, https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:1200:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 1200w, https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:1920:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 1920w, https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:2048:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 2048w, https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:3840:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp 3840w" src="https://img.decrypt.co/insecure/rs:fit:3840:0:0:0/plain/https://cdn.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png@webp"/><figcaption class="m-0 text-start gg-dark:text-neutral-200 scene:font-itc-avant-garde-gothic-pro scene:text-neutral-700 scene:font-light scene:mt-1">Joseph L.Popp aged 18. Image: Eastlake North High School yearbook</figcaption></figure><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Popp was arrested, but deemed mentally unfit to stand trial due to his increasingly </span><a href="https://medium.com/un-hackable/the-bizarre-pre-internet-history-of-ransomware-bb480a652b4b" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">strange behavior</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> (which included wearing condoms on his nose and putting curlers in his beard to ward off radiation.) He died in 2006 in a car accident and didn’t live to see his invention grow up, and—enhanced with a more effective method of encryption—become one of the world’s most prevalent cybercrimes. </span></p><h2 class="sc-b2a202e4-2 bmropA gg-dark:text-white scene:font-itc-avant-garde-gothic-pro scene:font-light" style="margin-top:2em;text-align:left;padding-bottom:16px;margin-bottom:16px;border-bottom:1px solid #dfe2e4" color="#333"><span style="font-weight:400">Ransomware and bitcoin</span></h2><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">For many years, however, ransomware languished as a small-time enterprise. It wasn’t until bitcoin began gaining traction, in 2012, that it really took off. Hackers fell in love with the decentralized digital currency, which made it difficult to trace or block payments, and it became ever easier to launder their ill-gotten gains as more cryptocurrencies hit the scene.</span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">“I don&#x27;t think there is much doubt that ransomware and cryptocurrencies go hand in hand,” </span><a href="https://www.dmu.ac.uk/about-dmu/academic-staff/business-and-law/edward-cartwright/edward-cartwright.aspx" target="_blank" rel="nofollow external noopener" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">Edward Cartwright</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">, Professor of Economics at De Montfort University, in the city of Leicester, UK, told </span><i><span style="font-weight:400">Decrypt</span></i><span style="font-weight:400">. “Ransomware is highly reliant on cryptocurrency and bitcoin in particular.”</span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><a href="https://www.coveware.com/blog/2019/4/15/ransom-amounts-rise-90-in-q1-as-ryuk-ransomware-increases?utm_source=emsisoft" target="_blank" rel="nofollow external noopener" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">Bitcoin accounted for about 98% of ransomware payments</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> made in the first quarter of 2019, according to data from Coveware. As a result, it’s become an </span><a href="https://blog.emsisoft.com/en/33977/is-ransomware-driving-up-the-price-of-bitcoin/" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">inextricable part</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> of the ransomware model.</span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">“Not only does it offer anonymity and untraceability to criminals it is also something that victims are willing to engage with, said Cartwright. </span></p><h2 class="sc-b2a202e4-2 bmropA gg-dark:text-white scene:font-itc-avant-garde-gothic-pro scene:font-light" style="margin-top:2em;text-align:left;padding-bottom:16px;margin-bottom:16px;border-bottom:1px solid #dfe2e4" color="#333"><span style="font-weight:400">The ransomware industry</span></h2><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Indeed, some experts say the increasing acceptance and understanding of cryptocurrency has driven ransomware from being a rarified crime into something far more common. </span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">“I strongly believe that cryptocurrency has played a role in the ransomware epidemic,” Victor Congionti cofounder and CEO of New York-based </span><a href="https://www.provendatarecovery.com/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow external noopener" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">Proven Data Recovery</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">, told </span><i><span style="font-weight:400">Decrypt</span></i><span style="font-weight:400">. </span></p><div class="post-content-w-full my-8 flex xl:hidden items-center justify-center"><div class="relative flex-none flex" style="min-width:300px;min-height:250px"></div></div><div class="post-content-w-full my-10 hidden xl:flex items-center justify-center"><div class="relative flex-none flex" style="min-width:300px;min-height:250px"></div></div><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Of course, in some cases, victims are able to catch intruders before ransomware has been activated or fully spread. In other cases, when the particular strain is “in the wild,” it may be possible to reverse engineer or create a “decryption utility,” Congionti said. But nine times out of ten the only way to reinstate files is to obtain decryption tools by paying the ransom, he added.  </span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Thus, a core service that Proven Data and other data recovery specialists offer is assisting victims willing to pay hackers’ bitcoin ransoms.</span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Anti-virus providers such as Emsisoft sometimes find ways to disable ransomware, and </span><a href="https://decrypt.co/10634/how-to-foil-a-ransomware-attack-without-paying-a-single-bitcoin" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">post those fixes online for free</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">. But they can decrypt ransomware </span><a href="https://www.theguardian.com/technology/2019/may/15/ransomware-samsam-payments-bitcoin-scam" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">only if there are errors in the underlying software</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> or if a security lapse allows the researchers to hack into the attacker’s server, otherwise, it’s essentially bulletproof.</span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">“The majority of cases require payment, because they’re using strong encryption. And there’s no other opinion than to pay or restore from backups,” said Congionti.</span></p><figure class="mt-8 mb-4 post-quote scene:pl-4 scene:md:pl-10 scene:border-l border-black"><blockquote><svg aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" data-prefix="fas" data-icon="quote-left" class="svg-inline--fa fa-quote-left fa-w-16 -mb-2 mt-2 opacity-30 hidden scene:block" role="img" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" viewBox="0 0 512 512"><path fill="currentColor" d="M464 256h-80v-64c0-35.3 28.7-64 64-64h8c13.3 0 24-10.7 24-24V56c0-13.3-10.7-24-24-24h-8c-88.4 0-160 71.6-160 160v240c0 26.5 21.5 48 48 48h128c26.5 0 48-21.5 48-48V304c0-26.5-21.5-48-48-48zm-288 0H96v-64c0-35.3 28.7-64 64-64h8c13.3 0 24-10.7 24-24V56c0-13.3-10.7-24-24-24h-8C71.6 32 0 103.6 0 192v240c0 26.5 21.5 48 48 48h128c26.5 0 48-21.5 48-48V304c0-26.5-21.5-48-48-48z"></path></svg><p class="scene:text-xl font-canela scene:font-itc-avant-garde-gothic-pro scene:font-medium font-black text-3xl md:text-4xl scene:md:text-2xl gg-dark:text-white">Ransomware has helped put bitcoin in the news and we know that the price of bitcoin goes up whenever it is in the news.</p><div class="flex justify-end"><svg aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" data-prefix="fas" data-icon="quote-right" class="svg-inline--fa fa-quote-right fa-w-16 mb-2 opacity-30 hidden scene:block" role="img" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" viewBox="0 0 512 512"><path fill="currentColor" d="M464 32H336c-26.5 0-48 21.5-48 48v128c0 26.5 21.5 48 48 48h80v64c0 35.3-28.7 64-64 64h-8c-13.3 0-24 10.7-24 24v48c0 13.3 10.7 24 24 24h8c88.4 0 160-71.6 160-160V80c0-26.5-21.5-48-48-48zm-288 0H48C21.5 32 0 53.5 0 80v128c0 26.5 21.5 48 48 48h80v64c0 35.3-28.7 64-64 64h-8c-13.3 0-24 10.7-24 24v48c0 13.3 10.7 24 24 24h8c88.4 0 160-71.6 160-160V80c0-26.5-21.5-48-48-48z"></path></svg></div></blockquote><figcaption class="text-start text-lg scene:font-itc-avant-garde-gothic-pro scene:font-light scene:text-right">—<!-- -->Edward Cartwright</figcaption></figure><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Since 2016, there have been around 4,000 ransomware attacks a day, amounting to </span><a href="https://www.propublica.org/article/sting-catches-another-ransomware-firm-red-mosquito-negotiating-with-hackers" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">1.5 million per yea</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">r, according to statistics posted by the US Department of Homeland Security. Little wonder then that firms like Proven Data have formed relationships with hackers, and can often </span><a href="https://www.propublica.org/article/the-extortion-economy-how-insurance-companies-are-fueling-a-rise-in-ransomware-attacks" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">negotiate the price down</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">. </span><a href="https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/gandcrab-ransomware-helps-shady-data-recovery-firms-hide-ransom-costs/" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">One hacker even offered data recovery firms exclusive “promo codes.”</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> They were told that after paying they’d receive a code for a discount on a future ransom. </span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Congionti said that simply paying the ransom is sometimes not enough. Hackers often provide decryption keys that contain corrupted data, or missing files, which then needs to be checked and reversioned in-house, </span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Their methods are also becoming increasingly sophisticated. Some have even initiated automated schemes via smart contracts that ensure decryption when a victim sends a payment. There’s no negotiating between humans; the crime is automated on the blockchain.</span></p><h2 class="sc-b2a202e4-2 bmropA gg-dark:text-white scene:font-itc-avant-garde-gothic-pro scene:font-light" style="margin-top:2em;text-align:left;padding-bottom:16px;margin-bottom:16px;border-bottom:1px solid #dfe2e4" color="#333"><span style="font-weight:400">Stockpiling bitcoin for ransom</span></h2><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">It can cost </span><a href="https://www.propublica.org/article/the-extortion-economy-how-insurance-companies-are-fueling-a-rise-in-ransomware-attacks" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">three times as much</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> to recover data than to pay the ransom. The speed of unlocking frozen accounts is often key for enterprises and organisations—for some, such as law firms, any downtime can be life threatening.</span></p><div class="post-content-w-full my-8 flex xl:hidden items-center justify-center"><div class="relative flex-none flex" style="min-width:300px;min-height:250px"></div></div><div class="post-content-w-full my-10 hidden xl:flex items-center justify-center"><div class="relative flex-none flex" style="min-width:300px;min-height:250px"></div></div><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">An October 2019 survey by data security startup Datto, polled 2,400 managed service providers, finding that </span><a href="https://www.datto.com/uk/blog/a-look-at-ransomware-in-2019" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">the average ransom attack cost $46,800 in downtime</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">—10 times the average ransom demand.</span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">As a result, companies such as Proven Data stockpile bitcoin for contingencies. “That’s part of the service—having that bitcoin readily available so there’s no delay in getting a company up and running as soon as possible.” said Congionti. </span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Another survey, in 2018, by security solutions provider Code24 suggested that victims were stockpiling cryptocurrency to minimize costs and disruption in the wake of a ransomware attack. The research found that almost </span><a href="https://on.code42.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Code42-Data-Exposure-Report-2-v11.pdf?utm_source=emsisoft" target="_blank" rel="nofollow external noopener" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">three-quarters</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> of Chief Information Security Officers chose to stash cryptocurrency for such an eventuality. But it’s notable that the study was conducted at the height of the cryptocurrency boom, when prices were marching ever upward.</span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">The policies of insurance companies may also be compounding the issue. Driven partly by the spread of ransomware, the cyber insurance market has </span><a href="https://www.propublica.org/article/the-extortion-economy-how-insurance-companies-are-fueling-a-rise-in-ransomware-attacks" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">grown rapidly</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">. Between 2015 and 2017, US cyber premiums doubled to an estimated $3.1 billion, according to the most recent data available.</span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Investigative non profit ProPublica published a report in August which found that </span><span style="font-weight:400">insurance companies are helping to pay ransoms</span><span style="font-weight:400">—inadvertently but essentially encouraging hackers to continue these attacks for profit. </span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Industry giant AIG reported in July that ransomware was its </span><a href="https://www.aig.co.uk/insights/claims-intelligence-cyber-report-2019" target="_blank" rel="nofollow external noopener" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">second leading cause of claims in 2018</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> and expected to increase in 2019. While the number of attacks had actually decreased, AIG said they have also become more costly, as the targets have become more specific. Criminals increasingly extort institutions that have deeper pockets and readily pay the ransom to minimize disruption to their operations</span></p><h2 class="sc-b2a202e4-2 bmropA gg-dark:text-white scene:font-itc-avant-garde-gothic-pro scene:font-light" style="margin-top:2em;text-align:left;padding-bottom:16px;margin-bottom:16px;border-bottom:1px solid #dfe2e4" color="#333"><span style="font-weight:400">Ransomware’s impact on bitcoin</span></h2><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Some analysts believe all this ransomware activity is bound to affect bitcoin’s price.</span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">“Ransomware has helped put bitcoin in the news and we know that the price of bitcoin goes up whenever it is in the news,” said De Montfort University’s Cartwright  “So, ransomware also partly drives the price of bitcoin.”</span></p><div class="post-content-w-full my-8 flex xl:hidden items-center justify-center"><div class="relative flex-none flex" style="min-width:300px;min-height:250px"></div></div><div class="post-content-w-full my-10 hidden xl:flex items-center justify-center"><div class="relative flex-none flex" style="min-width:300px;min-height:250px"></div></div><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Cartwright believes that the effect of a ransomware attack is significant enough to warrant inclusion in any algorithmic trading model that factors in external events, thus taking advantage of prospective price movement in the wake of an attack. </span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">But that doesn’t help local governments, businesses and law enforcement agencies, who are </span><a href="https://www.cnet.com/news/ransomware-devastated-cities-in-2019-officials-hope-to-stop-a-repeat-in-2020/" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">desperate for solutions</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> to ransomware attacks that threaten to cripple them. </span></p><figure class="w-full max-w-full mt-4 overflow-hidden"><img alt="RYUK" loading="lazy" width="704" height="400" decoding="async" data-nimg="1" class="object-contain object-center w-full" style="color:transparent" sizes="(min-width: Image: Flickr</figcaption></figure><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Last summer, in response to hackers demands for millions of dollars, a coalition of </span><a href="https://www.cnet.com/news/us-mayors-adopt-resolution-to-not-pay-hackers-over-ransomware-attacks/" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">227 US mayors vowed not to pay</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">. Which might well be the best solution.</span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Data recovery experts, including Proven Data, report that ransomware attacks increasingly show the characteristics of organized cybercrime, and fear that many ransom payments end up in the hands of terrorist groups. Through paying a ransom, local governments are inadvertently funding them.</span></p><h2 class="sc-b2a202e4-2 bmropA gg-dark:text-white scene:font-itc-avant-garde-gothic-pro scene:font-light" style="margin-top:2em;text-align:left;padding-bottom:16px;margin-bottom:16px;border-bottom:1px solid #dfe2e4" color="#333"><span style="font-weight:400">A concerted attack</span></h2><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Government officials hope that, though better security, they can properly protect cities from these kinds of attacks. Congionti suggested that the government should make it mandatory for businesses to go through some basic security protocols, as well.</span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">And this year, the White House and U.S. Senate </span><a href="https://www.congress.gov/bill/116th-congress/house-bill/1158/actions" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">approved versions of a bill</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> that would allow the Department of Home Security to invest in resources to help states and cities deal more effectively with ransomware attacks.</span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Either way, a policy of not paying ransom ought to help eradicate the scourge of ransomware.</span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">But for now,  RYUK, a particularly robust ransomware that can sometimes even find and destroy backups, is on the rise. It’s named after the god of death in the anime, </span><i><span style="font-weight:400">Death Note</span></i><span style="font-weight:400">, and is believed to have originated in North Korea. </span></p><div class="post-content-w-full my-8 flex xl:hidden items-center justify-center"><div class="relative flex-none flex" style="min-width:300px;min-height:250px"></div></div><div class="post-content-w-full my-10 hidden xl:flex items-center justify-center"><div class="relative flex-none flex" style="min-width:300px;min-height:250px"></div></div><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">Over the first five months of 2019, RYUK </span><a href="https://www.zdnet.com/article/over-500-us-schools-were-hit-by-ransomware-in-2019/" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">hit more than 500 schools</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> and earned</span><a href="https://thenextweb.com/hardfork/2019/01/14/ryuk-bitcoin-ransomware/" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400"> hackers more than $3 million</span></a><span style="font-weight:400"> in bitcoin. And security experts expect it, and new ransomware attacks against local governments,</span> <a href="https://decrypt.co/14321/south-koreas-security-agencies-predict-more-hacks-in-2020" target="_blank" class="sc-adb616fe-0 bJsyml"><span style="font-weight:400">will only ramp up in 2020</span></a><span style="font-weight:400">. </span></p><p class="font-meta-serif-pro scene:font-noto-sans scene:text-base scene:md:text-lg font-normal text-lg md:text-xl md:leading-9 tracking-px text-body gg-dark:text-neutral-100"><span style="font-weight:400">At the ripe adult age of 30, Popp’s invention is adept at outrunning most efforts to thwart it. This is not a happy birthday</span></p><div class="my-4 border-b border-decryptGridline"><div class="text-start p-8 md:py-12 md:px-12 max-w-prose relative"><span class="border-t-4 border-l-4 w-4 h-4 And the malicious software, invented by the well meaning but wacko evolutionary biologist Joseph L. Popp, is thriving. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eAttacks spiked by \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.mcafee.com/enterprise/en-us/assets/reports/rp-quarterly-threats-aug-2019.pdf\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e118%\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e during the first quarter of this year, with \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://blog.barracuda.com/2019/08/28/threat-spotlight-government-ransomware-attacks\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003ehackers singling out for punishment\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e state and local governments, while continuing to target \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.cnet.com/news/wannacry-ransomware-real-victim-small-business-local-corner-store/\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003ebusinesses\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e, \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.cnet.com/news/us-charges-iranian-hackers-over-ransomware-attacks-on-major-cities/\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003euniversities\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e, and \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://threatpost.com/alabama-hospitals-pay-up-ransomware-attack/148937/\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003ehospitals\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eRansomware’s robust health is due to three symbiotic factors: our increasing reliance on digitization; ever more sophisticated crooks delivering more powerful viral strains, and the prevalence of untraceable ransoms—now almost always paid in bitcoin or other cryptocurrencies. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eHackers’ demands are also increasing along with the chilling efficacy of their product. According to ransomware recovery specialists \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.coveware.com/\" data-wpel-link=\"external\" rel=\"nofollow external noopener\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eCoveware\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e, the average ransom payment \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.coveware.com/blog/2019/7/15/ransomware-amounts-rise-3x-in-q2-as-ryuk-amp-sodinokibi-spread?utm_source=emsisoft\" data-wpel-link=\"external\" rel=\"nofollow external noopener\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eincreased by 184%\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e \u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e in the first half of 2019. Largely, that’s thanks to an increasing number of attacks with new ransomware strains such as RYUK on large enterprises. \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.datto.com/uk/blog/a-look-at-ransomware-in-2019\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eThe average ransom demanded\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e, internationally, is now $4,300. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eDesperate for a quick solution, most victims pay up, data recovery professionals told \u003c/span\u003e\u003ci\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eDecrypt\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/i\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e. In fact, \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://on.code42.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Code42-Data-Exposure-Report-2-v11.pdf?utm_source=emsisoft\" data-wpel-link=\"external\" rel=\"nofollow external noopener\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eaccording to one report\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e, many businesses have begun hoarding cryptocurrencies, in case of an attack. Is it any wonder then that \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://blog.emsisoft.com/en/33977/is-ransomware-driving-up-the-price-of-bitcoin/\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003esome analysts\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e believe major ransomware attacks could be affecting the price of cryptocurrency? \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eHappy Birthday ransomware\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eRansomware refers to the category of computer viruses that are designed to quickly across computer networks and encrypt the files on them; the idea is to hold sensitive documents hostage until the victim pays ransom to the hacker. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eThe vulnerability of those targeted—\u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://decrypt.co/12481/bitcoin-ransomware-paralyzes-nursing-homes-across-the-us\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003enursing homes\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e, \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://decrypt.co/10389/ransomware-attacks-targeting-local-infrastructure-new-research-shows\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eproviders of local infrastructure\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e, and \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://decrypt.co/7651/second-florida-city-gives-in-to-bitcoin-ransom-pays-out-500000\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003ecities\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e—gives them little alternative. In May, an RYUK attack on the City of Riviera Beach, Florida, \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://statescoop.com/florida-city-pays-hackers-600000-after-ransomware-attack/\" data-wpel-link=\"external\" rel=\"nofollow external noopener\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eforced the local government to cough up $600,000\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e to decrypt the frozen files. In October, \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://thenextweb.com/hardfork/2019/10/25/hackers-give-johannesburg-government-3-days-to-pay-over-30k-in-bitcoin/\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003ehackers hit \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003ethe administrative website of the City of Johannesburg, in South Africa, and threatened to publish the stolen data on the Internet—unless they received a $30,000 bitcoin ransom. The city \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://threatpost.com/johannesburg-refuses-ransom-pay/149676/\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003erefused\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e to pay.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eBut as bad as the blight is, ransomware wasn’t born bad. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eHarvard-educated Popp, its inventor, was a polymath, and ransomware was born in 1989 out of his desire to combat AIDS, or \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.theatlantic.com/technology/archive/2016/05/the-computer-virus-that-haunted-early-aids-researchers/481965/\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eso he claimed\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e. In his misguided determination to amass funds to thwart the disease, he mailed more than 20,000 infected floppy disks to the delegate list of a World Health Organisation forum. When the recipients ran the disks, their computers froze, and an onscreen message instructed them to send funds to access a second disk that would restore their files. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cfigure id=\"attachment_15405\" aria-describedby=\"caption-attachment-15405\" style=\"width: 240px\" class=\"wp-caption aligncenter\"\u003e\u003cimg loading=\"lazy\" class=\"size-full wp-image-15405\" src=\"https://wp.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png\" alt=\"Joseph L. Popp\" width=\"240\" height=\"328\" srcset=\"https://wp.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp.png 240w, https://wp.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/popp-popp-220x300.png 220w\" sizes=\"(max-width: 240px) 100vw, 240px\" /\u003e\u003cfigcaption id=\"caption-attachment-15405\" class=\"wp-caption-text\"\u003eJoseph L.Popp aged 18. Image: Eastlake North High School yearbook\u003c/figcaption\u003e\u003c/figure\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003ePopp was arrested, but deemed mentally unfit to stand trial due to his increasingly \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://medium.com/un-hackable/the-bizarre-pre-internet-history-of-ransomware-bb480a652b4b\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003estrange behavior\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e (which included wearing condoms on his nose and putting curlers in his beard to ward off radiation.) He died in 2006 in a car accident and didn’t live to see his invention grow up, and—enhanced with a more effective method of encryption—become one of the world’s most prevalent cybercrimes. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eRansomware and bitcoin\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eFor many years, however, ransomware languished as a small-time enterprise. It wasn’t until bitcoin began gaining traction, in 2012, that it really took off. Hackers fell in love with the decentralized digital currency, which made it difficult to trace or block payments, and it became ever easier to launder their ill-gotten gains as more cryptocurrencies hit the scene.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e“I don't think there is much doubt that ransomware and cryptocurrencies go hand in hand,” \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.dmu.ac.uk/about-dmu/academic-staff/business-and-law/edward-cartwright/edward-cartwright.aspx\" data-wpel-link=\"external\" rel=\"nofollow external noopener\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eEdward Cartwright\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e, Professor of Economics at De Montfort University, in the city of Leicester, UK, told \u003c/span\u003e\u003ci\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eDecrypt\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/i\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e. “Ransomware is highly reliant on cryptocurrency and bitcoin in particular.”\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.coveware.com/blog/2019/4/15/ransom-amounts-rise-90-in-q1-as-ryuk-ransomware-increases?utm_source=emsisoft\" data-wpel-link=\"external\" rel=\"nofollow external noopener\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eBitcoin accounted for about 98% of ransomware payments\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e made in the first quarter of 2019, according to data from Coveware. As a result, it’s become an \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://blog.emsisoft.com/en/33977/is-ransomware-driving-up-the-price-of-bitcoin/\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003einextricable part\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e of the ransomware model.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e“Not only does it offer anonymity and untraceability to criminals it is also something that victims are willing to engage with, said Cartwright. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eThe ransomware industry\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eIndeed, some experts say the increasing acceptance and understanding of cryptocurrency has driven ransomware from being a rarified crime into something far more common. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e“I strongly believe that cryptocurrency has played a role in the ransomware epidemic,” Victor Congionti cofounder and CEO of New York-based \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.provendatarecovery.com/\" data-wpel-link=\"external\" rel=\"nofollow external noopener\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eProven Data Recovery\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e, told \u003c/span\u003e\u003ci\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eDecrypt\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/i\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eOf course, in some cases, victims are able to catch intruders before ransomware has been activated or fully spread. In other cases, when the particular strain is “in the wild,” it may be possible to reverse engineer or create a “decryption utility,” Congionti said. But nine times out of ten the only way to reinstate files is to obtain decryption tools by paying the ransom, he added.  \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eThus, a core service that Proven Data and other data recovery specialists offer is assisting victims willing to pay hackers’ bitcoin ransoms.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eAnti-virus providers such as Emsisoft sometimes find ways to disable ransomware, and \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://decrypt.co/10634/how-to-foil-a-ransomware-attack-without-paying-a-single-bitcoin\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003epost those fixes online for free\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e. But they can decrypt ransomware \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.theguardian.com/technology/2019/may/15/ransomware-samsam-payments-bitcoin-scam\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eonly if there are errors in the underlying software\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e or if a security lapse allows the researchers to hack into the attacker’s server, otherwise, it’s essentially bulletproof.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e“The majority of cases require payment, because they’re using strong encryption. And there’s no other opinion than to pay or restore from backups,” said Congionti.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e\u003cdiv class=\"pull-quote\" data-attribution=\"Edward Cartwright\"\u003eRansomware has helped put bitcoin in the news and we know that the price of bitcoin goes up whenever it is in the news.\u003c/div\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eSince 2016, there have been around 4,000 ransomware attacks a day, amounting to \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.propublica.org/article/sting-catches-another-ransomware-firm-red-mosquito-negotiating-with-hackers\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e1.5 million per yea\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003er, according to statistics posted by the US Department of Homeland Security. Little wonder then that firms like Proven Data have formed relationships with hackers, and can often \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.propublica.org/article/the-extortion-economy-how-insurance-companies-are-fueling-a-rise-in-ransomware-attacks\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003enegotiate the price down\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e. \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/gandcrab-ransomware-helps-shady-data-recovery-firms-hide-ransom-costs/\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eOne hacker even offered data recovery firms exclusive “promo codes.”\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e They were told that after paying they’d receive a code for a discount on a future ransom. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eCongionti said that simply paying the ransom is sometimes not enough. Hackers often provide decryption keys that contain corrupted data, or missing files, which then needs to be checked and reversioned in-house, \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eTheir methods are also becoming increasingly sophisticated. Some have even initiated automated schemes via smart contracts that ensure decryption when a victim sends a payment. There’s no negotiating between humans; the crime is automated on the blockchain.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eStockpiling bitcoin for ransom\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eIt can cost \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.propublica.org/article/the-extortion-economy-how-insurance-companies-are-fueling-a-rise-in-ransomware-attacks\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003ethree times as much\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e to recover data than to pay the ransom. The speed of unlocking frozen accounts is often key for enterprises and organisations—for some, such as law firms, any downtime can be life threatening.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eAn October 2019 survey by data security startup Datto, polled 2,400 managed service providers, finding that \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.datto.com/uk/blog/a-look-at-ransomware-in-2019\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003ethe average ransom attack cost $46,800 in downtime\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e—10 times the average ransom demand.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eAs a result, companies such as Proven Data stockpile bitcoin for contingencies. “That’s part of the service—having that bitcoin readily available so there’s no delay in getting a company up and running as soon as possible.” said Congionti. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eAnother survey, in 2018, by security solutions provider Code24 suggested that victims were stockpiling cryptocurrency to minimize costs and disruption in the wake of a ransomware attack. The research found that almost \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://on.code42.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Code42-Data-Exposure-Report-2-v11.pdf?utm_source=emsisoft\" data-wpel-link=\"external\" rel=\"nofollow external noopener\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003ethree-quarters\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e of Chief Information Security Officers chose to stash cryptocurrency for such an eventuality. But it’s notable that the study was conducted at the height of the cryptocurrency boom, when prices were marching ever upward.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e\u003cdiv class=\"embedded-post\" data-id=\"10660\" data-post-title=\"Monero more dangerous than Bitcoin, says German Finance Ministry \" data-post-type=\"post\" data-post-orientation=\"horizontal\"\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://wp.decrypt.co/10660/monero-more-dangerous-than-bitcoin-says-german-finance-ministry/\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003eMonero more dangerous than Bitcoin, says German Finance Ministry \u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eThe policies of insurance companies may also be compounding the issue. Driven partly by the spread of ransomware, the cyber insurance market has \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.propublica.org/article/the-extortion-economy-how-insurance-companies-are-fueling-a-rise-in-ransomware-attacks\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003egrown rapidly\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e. Between 2015 and 2017, US cyber premiums doubled to an estimated $3.1 billion, according to the most recent data available.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eInvestigative non profit ProPublica published a report in August which found that \u003c/span\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003einsurance companies are helping to pay ransoms\u003c/span\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e—inadvertently but essentially encouraging hackers to continue these attacks for profit. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eIndustry giant AIG reported in July that ransomware was its \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.aig.co.uk/insights/claims-intelligence-cyber-report-2019\" data-wpel-link=\"external\" rel=\"nofollow external noopener\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003esecond leading cause of claims in 2018\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e and expected to increase in 2019. While the number of attacks had actually decreased, AIG said they have also become more costly, as the targets have become more specific. Criminals increasingly extort institutions that have deeper pockets and readily pay the ransom to minimize disruption to their operations\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eRansomware’s impact on bitcoin\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eSome analysts believe all this ransomware activity is bound to affect bitcoin’s price.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e“Ransomware has helped put bitcoin in the news and we know that the price of bitcoin goes up whenever it is in the news,” said De Montfort University’s Cartwright  “So, ransomware also partly drives the price of bitcoin.”\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eCartwright believes that the effect of a ransomware attack is significant enough to warrant inclusion in any algorithmic trading model that factors in external events, thus taking advantage of prospective price movement in the wake of an attack. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eBut that doesn’t help local governments, businesses and law enforcement agencies, who are \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.cnet.com/news/ransomware-devastated-cities-in-2019-officials-hope-to-stop-a-repeat-in-2020/\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003edesperate for solutions\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e to ransomware attacks that threaten to cripple them. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cfigure id=\"attachment_15414\" aria-describedby=\"caption-attachment-15414\" style=\"width: 704px\" class=\"wp-caption aligncenter\"\u003e\u003cimg loading=\"lazy\" class=\"size-full wp-image-15414\" src=\"https://wp.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/2318629899_0991efc170_o.jpg\" alt=\"RYUK\" width=\"704\" height=\"400\" srcset=\"https://wp.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/2318629899_0991efc170_o.jpg 704w, https://wp.decrypt.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/2318629899_0991efc170_o-300x170.jpg 300w\" sizes=\"(max-width: 704px) 100vw, 704px\" /\u003e\u003cfigcaption id=\"caption-attachment-15414\" class=\"wp-caption-text\"\u003eRYUK ransomware is named after the god of death in the anime Death Note. Image: Flickr\u003c/figcaption\u003e\u003c/figure\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eLast summer, in response to hackers demands for millions of dollars, a coalition of \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.cnet.com/news/us-mayors-adopt-resolution-to-not-pay-hackers-over-ransomware-attacks/\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e227 US mayors vowed not to pay\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e. Which might well be the best solution.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eData recovery experts, including Proven Data, report that ransomware attacks increasingly show the characteristics of organized cybercrime, and fear that many ransom payments end up in the hands of terrorist groups. Through paying a ransom, local governments are inadvertently funding them.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eA concerted attack\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eGovernment officials hope that, though better security, they can properly protect cities from these kinds of attacks. Congionti suggested that the government should make it mandatory for businesses to go through some basic security protocols, as well.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eAnd this year, the White House and U.S. Senate \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.congress.gov/bill/116th-congress/house-bill/1158/actions\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eapproved versions of a bill\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e that would allow the Department of Home Security to invest in resources to help states and cities deal more effectively with ransomware attacks.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eEither way, a policy of not paying ransom ought to help eradicate the scourge of ransomware.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eBut for now,  RYUK, a particularly robust ransomware that can sometimes even find and destroy backups, is on the rise. It’s named after the god of death in the anime, \u003c/span\u003e\u003ci\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eDeath Note\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/i\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e, and is believed to have originated in North Korea. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eOver the first five months of 2019, RYUK \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.zdnet.com/article/over-500-us-schools-were-hit-by-ransomware-in-2019/\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003ehit more than 500 schools\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e and earned\u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://thenextweb.com/hardfork/2019/01/14/ryuk-bitcoin-ransomware/\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e hackers more than $3 million\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e in bitcoin. And security experts expect it, and new ransomware attacks against local governments,\u003c/span\u003e \u003ca href=\"https://decrypt.co/14321/south-koreas-security-agencies-predict-more-hacks-in-2020\" data-wpel-link=\"internal\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003ewill only ramp up in 2020\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003e. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight: 400;\"\u003eAt the ripe adult age of 30, Popp’s invention is adept at outrunning most efforts to thwart it.
